What a run is. One invocation of the framework on one design with one mode: here raygentop with comb_sop_2_18_mode.

What happened. 2 rewrites measured (Pass 1, Pass 2); kept: Pass 1 (the lowest area x delay, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%). Loop stopped by: no gain beyond noise (gain -1.91% against first, min-gain 0.046 (baseline seed_figures (area x delay over the entry's seed runs))).

Result. Area x delay +2.16% against the unmodified design on the same layout (koios_extra_small, 90 x 90): 2.3559e+07 vs 2.30616e+07 (baseline: median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them)); verification: not recorded in the run record.

Mode contract

recorded (outcome.json contract field)

Verbatim, as recorded with the run and sent to the agents.

- primitive to instantiate: `comb_sop_2_18` (precision INT19)
- computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
- input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
- output ports: result[64]
- arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
- registers: none; the block is combinational, and `result` is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
- connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.

8 call(s) in 2 passes, in order

prompt
Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = ul (8b, zero-extend to 18), ay = rul (7b, zero-extend to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = rvl (7b → 19), result → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g: ax = ul, ay = gul, bx = vl, by = gvl, result → new reg i12g
  • block sop_b: ax = ul, ay = bul, bx = vl, by = bvl, result → new reg i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What changes — in module bilinearintrp (lines 2762–2887 of raygentop.v) only. Nothing outside the module is touched.

Each colour channel currently is: nine registered products (lines 2874–2882), then a 3-term combinational sum truncated to 7 bits (lines 2884–2886). For each channel, the two products on ul and vl move into one comb_sop_2_18, whose combinational sum is registered in place of the two separate product registers. The wl product keeps its soft multiplier and its register, and the final adder becomes a 2-term add.

Edits

  1. Delete the declarations of i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2799–2807 keep only i3b, i3g, i3r). Add:
    ```verilog
    reg [15:0] i12r;
    reg [15:0] i12g;
    reg [15:0] i12b;
    wire [63:0] sopr, sopg, sopb;
    ```
  2. Instantiate the three blocks at module scope (outside the always blocks), with explicit zero-extension so the unsigned widening is visible:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, rul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, rvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, gul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, gvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, bul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, bvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```
  3. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883): delete lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881; keep 2876, 2879, 2882 (i3r <= wl * rwl; i3g <= wl * gwl; i3b <= wl * bwl;) exactly as they are; add
    ```verilog
    i12r <= sopr[15:0];
    i12g <= sopg[15:0];
    i12b <= sopb[15:0];
    ```
    Keep every other assignment in that block (wl, ul, vl, rul … bwl) unchanged — they are the block's operands and wl/ul/vl have other readers.
  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r + i3r);
    assign g = (i12g + i3g);
    assign b = (i12b + i3b);
    ```

Why this is the same behaviour

  • Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bwl), matching the block's unsigned reading; zero-extension to 18/19 bits is value-preserving. Each product is at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum at most 64770, so the 64-bit result is exact and sopX[15:0] is the full sum — no wrap, no saturation to consider.
  • Timing: at a clock edge in cycle t, sopr is a pure function of the pre-edge ul, rul, vl, rvl, exactly what lines 2874–2875 multiplied, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1). i3r is untouched. Hence r = i12r + i3r equals i1r + i2r + i3r on every cycle, truncated to 7 bits identically (truncation is distributive over addition mod 2^7).
  • Visibility: i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are read nowhere except lines 2884–2886 (checked across the whole file), so folding them into the blocks hides nothing another reader needs. ul, vl and the colour registers stay outside the blocks as real registers, so their other readers are unaffected.
  • Registers: the block has none and needs no reset; i12r/i12g/i12b replace i1*/i2* at the same pipeline position. They power up unknown, but the module has no reset port and the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in clocks them to a defined value before comparison starts (comparison begins at the end of the lead-in), and they resolve no later than the original i1r/i2r did.
  • No assumption about the design's inputs is required; there is no ASSUMES: line.

Register stages, per converted region — before: operands ul/vl/rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*) → combinational sum → output. After: operands → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational add with i3* → output. One stage before and one after; output latency from module inputs is unchanged.

Candidates left unconverted — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl products). Each is the third product of a 3-product chain whose first two already fill one block. Putting each in a block of its own would need a second DSP tile per channel (253779 MWTA ≈ 9.1 CLBs) to absorb one 8×7 multiply plus the 7-bit final add — less soft logic than a DSP tile costs — and it would place a 2.14 ns combinational block between the i12* registers and the module outputs r/g/b, which drive combinational mux logic in the parent (lines 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484) before reaching a register, lengthening that stage. Both effects push area × delay the wrong way, so they stay in soft logic.

Block count — the rewrite adds 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of the 132 available. It removes six soft 8×7 multipliers, three 15-bit adder inputs (the sums go from 3-term to 2-term) and 42 flip-flops (six 15-bit product registers replaced by three 16-bit registers), which is more soft area than the three DSP tiles cost. The longest new path is ul/rul flop → block → i12* flop ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 = 2.20 ns, ending at a register and shorter than the 16×16 soft multiplies in matmult (lines 335–343) that set this design's critical path, so clock period should not regress.

DESIGN_CHANGE: matmult (lines 335–350) computes nine 16×16 products summed three per output (Cx, Cy, Cz); those operands fit ax[18]/ay[19] directly and are the design's dominant arithmetic and likely critical path. Exposing them as candidate regions would let the block displace far more soft logic than bilinearintrp's 8×7 products do.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is raygentop.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id

bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = ul (8b, zero-extend to 18), ay = rul (7b, zero-extend to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = rvl (7b → 19), result → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g: ax = ul, ay = gul, bx = vl, by = gvl, result → new reg i12g
  • block sop_b: ax = ul, ay = bul, bx = vl, by = bvl, result → new reg i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What changes — in module bilinearintrp (lines 2762–2887 of raygentop.v) only. Nothing outside the module is touched.

Each colour channel currently is: nine registered products (lines 2874–2882), then a 3-term combinational sum truncated to 7 bits (lines 2884–2886). For each channel, the two products on ul and vl move into one comb_sop_2_18, whose combinational sum is registered in place of the two separate product registers. The wl product keeps its soft multiplier and its register, and the final adder becomes a 2-term add.

Edits

  1. Delete the declarations of i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2799–2807 keep only i3b, i3g, i3r). Add:
    ```verilog
    reg [15:0] i12r;
    reg [15:0] i12g;
    reg [15:0] i12b;
    wire [63:0] sopr, sopg, sopb;
    ```
  2. Instantiate the three blocks at module scope (outside the always blocks), with explicit zero-extension so the unsigned widening is visible:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, rul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, rvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, gul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, gvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, bul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, bvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```
  3. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883): delete lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881; keep 2876, 2879, 2882 (i3r <= wl * rwl; i3g <= wl * gwl; i3b <= wl * bwl;) exactly as they are; add
    ```verilog
    i12r <= sopr[15:0];
    i12g <= sopg[15:0];
    i12b <= sopb[15:0];
    ```
    Keep every other assignment in that block (wl, ul, vl, rul … bwl) unchanged — they are the block's operands and wl/ul/vl have other readers.
  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r + i3r);
    assign g = (i12g + i3g);
    assign b = (i12b + i3b);
    ```

Why this is the same behaviour

  • Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bwl), matching the block's unsigned reading; zero-extension to 18/19 bits is value-preserving. Each product is at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum at most 64770, so the 64-bit result is exact and sopX[15:0] is the full sum — no wrap, no saturation to consider.
  • Timing: at a clock edge in cycle t, sopr is a pure function of the pre-edge ul, rul, vl, rvl, exactly what lines 2874–2875 multiplied, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1). i3r is untouched. Hence r = i12r + i3r equals i1r + i2r + i3r on every cycle, truncated to 7 bits identically (truncation is distributive over addition mod 2^7).
  • Visibility: i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are read nowhere except lines 2884–2886 (checked across the whole file), so folding them into the blocks hides nothing another reader needs. ul, vl and the colour registers stay outside the blocks as real registers, so their other readers are unaffected.
  • Registers: the block has none and needs no reset; i12r/i12g/i12b replace i1*/i2* at the same pipeline position. They power up unknown, but the module has no reset port and the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in clocks them to a defined value before comparison starts (comparison begins at the end of the lead-in), and they resolve no later than the original i1r/i2r did.
  • No assumption about the design's inputs is required; there is no ASSUMES: line.

Register stages, per converted region — before: operands ul/vl/rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*) → combinational sum → output. After: operands → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational add with i3* → output. One stage before and one after; output latency from module inputs is unchanged.

Candidates left unconverted — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl products). Each is the third product of a 3-product chain whose first two already fill one block. Putting each in a block of its own would need a second DSP tile per channel (253779 MWTA ≈ 9.1 CLBs) to absorb one 8×7 multiply plus the 7-bit final add — less soft logic than a DSP tile costs — and it would place a 2.14 ns combinational block between the i12* registers and the module outputs r/g/b, which drive combinational mux logic in the parent (lines 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484) before reaching a register, lengthening that stage. Both effects push area × delay the wrong way, so they stay in soft logic.

Block count — the rewrite adds 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of the 132 available. It removes six soft 8×7 multipliers, three 15-bit adder inputs (the sums go from 3-term to 2-term) and 42 flip-flops (six 15-bit product registers replaced by three 16-bit registers), which is more soft area than the three DSP tiles cost. The longest new path is ul/rul flop → block → i12* flop ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 = 2.20 ns, ending at a register and shorter than the 16×16 soft multiplies in matmult (lines 335–343) that set this design's critical path, so clock period should not regress.

DESIGN_CHANGE: matmult (lines 335–350) computes nine 16×16 products summed three per output (Cx, Cy, Cz); those operands fit ax[18]/ay[19] directly and are the design's dominant arithmetic and likely critical path. Exposing them as candidate regions would let the block displace far more soft logic than bilinearintrp's 8×7 products do.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

I'll read the design source.

  • Visibility — i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are declared at raygentop.v:2799–2806, written only at raygentop.v:2874–2881, and read only at raygentop.v:2884–2886. No other reader in the file, so folding them into block outputs hides nothing. ul/vl/wl and the colour registers remain real registers outside the blocks.
  • Widths / arithmetic — ul,vl are reg[7:0] (raygentop.v:2796–2797), rul…bwl are reg[6:0] (raygentop.v:2808–2816); all unsigned, so {10'b0,ul} (18b) and {12'b0,rul} (19b) are value-preserving. Each product ≤ 255·127 = 32385, so the original reg[14:0] products never truncate; the pair sum ≤ 64770 fits the new reg[15:0], and sopr[15:0] is exact — no 64-bit wrap to handle. The final assign r = (i12r + i3r) evaluates at 16 bits then truncates to r[6:0]; the original i1r+i2r+i3r evaluates at 15 bits then truncates to 7. Both reduce to the same value mod 2^7.
  • Timing/edge semantics — sopr is a continuous function of the pre-edge ul, vl, rul, rvl; the non-blocking RHS at the posedge clk block (raygentop.v:2860) samples the same pre-edge values the deleted lines did, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1) cycle for cycle. i3* untouched.
  • Registers/reset — the block is combinational with no reset, consistent with sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v. bilinearintrp has no reset port (raygentop.v:2762); tb_equiv_bilin.v:216–219 runs an 8-cycle all-zero lead-in with cmp = 0 and only then compares, and i12* resolve on the first posedge, so the added registers' unknown start-up is flushed before comparison. No ASSUMES: line is needed, and none is claimed.
  • Terms / ports — all four product-term ports are driven on each of the three instances by name; nothing tied to 0, nothing dropped. The wl* terms stay in soft logic outside the blocks, which keeps every term of the original sums.
  • Cited lines in the left-region reason — blr/blg/blb (raygentop.v:1990–1992, instance at 2012) are read at 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484 as cited; the matmult products in the DESIGN_CHANGE note are at raygentop.v:335–350 as cited. The note proposes no edit, so scope is unchanged.
  • Scope — edits confined to bilinearintrp (raygentop.v:2762–2887); one instance of the module exists, matching the candidate table.

Call 3 · editor · anthropic/claude-opus-5 · ok in 26 · out 5145 · reasoning 841 · cache read 257615 · cache write 20030 · 78.0 s
prompt
Task

Edit raygentop.v in this directory to carry out the approved plan below: rewrite the regions it names onto the hard block described next. If a step cannot be done as the plan says, stop and say which step and why; do not improvise a different rewrite.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
  • Regions in the approved plan: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355. The plan names their shared sum as 'module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)'. In the design their products are registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
How to work
  • After each edit, run bash check.sh raygentop.v and read its output. If it fails, fix the RTL and run it again.
  • check.sh reads the architecture's declaration of every hard block. Instantiate the block by its name and do not declare it in the design: a design that defines a module of the block's name fails the check.
  • Do not run place-and-route or any VTR command; that is done after you finish.
  • Stop when check.sh passes and its cell census shows the block instances the plan calls for.
Output
  • State what you changed, region by region, and stop.
  • Your edit is checked automatically, and this reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = ul (8b, zero-extend to 18), ay = rul (7b, zero-extend to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = rvl (7b → 19), result → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g: ax = ul, ay = gul, bx = vl, by = gvl, result → new reg i12g
  • block sop_b: ax = ul, ay = bul, bx = vl, by = bvl, result → new reg i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What changes — in module bilinearintrp (lines 2762–2887 of raygentop.v) only. Nothing outside the module is touched.

Each colour channel currently is: nine registered products (lines 2874–2882), then a 3-term combinational sum truncated to 7 bits (lines 2884–2886). For each channel, the two products on ul and vl move into one comb_sop_2_18, whose combinational sum is registered in place of the two separate product registers. The wl product keeps its soft multiplier and its register, and the final adder becomes a 2-term add.

Edits

  1. Delete the declarations of i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2799–2807 keep only i3b, i3g, i3r). Add:
    ```verilog
    reg [15:0] i12r;
    reg [15:0] i12g;
    reg [15:0] i12b;
    wire [63:0] sopr, sopg, sopb;
    ```
  2. Instantiate the three blocks at module scope (outside the always blocks), with explicit zero-extension so the unsigned widening is visible:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, rul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, rvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, gul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, gvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, bul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, bvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```
  3. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883): delete lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881; keep 2876, 2879, 2882 (i3r <= wl * rwl; i3g <= wl * gwl; i3b <= wl * bwl;) exactly as they are; add
    ```verilog
    i12r <= sopr[15:0];
    i12g <= sopg[15:0];
    i12b <= sopb[15:0];
    ```
    Keep every other assignment in that block (wl, ul, vl, rul … bwl) unchanged — they are the block's operands and wl/ul/vl have other readers.
  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r + i3r);
    assign g = (i12g + i3g);
    assign b = (i12b + i3b);
    ```

Why this is the same behaviour

  • Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bwl), matching the block's unsigned reading; zero-extension to 18/19 bits is value-preserving. Each product is at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum at most 64770, so the 64-bit result is exact and sopX[15:0] is the full sum — no wrap, no saturation to consider.
  • Timing: at a clock edge in cycle t, sopr is a pure function of the pre-edge ul, rul, vl, rvl, exactly what lines 2874–2875 multiplied, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1). i3r is untouched. Hence r = i12r + i3r equals i1r + i2r + i3r on every cycle, truncated to 7 bits identically (truncation is distributive over addition mod 2^7).
  • Visibility: i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are read nowhere except lines 2884–2886 (checked across the whole file), so folding them into the blocks hides nothing another reader needs. ul, vl and the colour registers stay outside the blocks as real registers, so their other readers are unaffected.
  • Registers: the block has none and needs no reset; i12r/i12g/i12b replace i1*/i2* at the same pipeline position. They power up unknown, but the module has no reset port and the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in clocks them to a defined value before comparison starts (comparison begins at the end of the lead-in), and they resolve no later than the original i1r/i2r did.
  • No assumption about the design's inputs is required; there is no ASSUMES: line.

Register stages, per converted region — before: operands ul/vl/rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*) → combinational sum → output. After: operands → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational add with i3* → output. One stage before and one after; output latency from module inputs is unchanged.

Candidates left unconverted — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl products). Each is the third product of a 3-product chain whose first two already fill one block. Putting each in a block of its own would need a second DSP tile per channel (253779 MWTA ≈ 9.1 CLBs) to absorb one 8×7 multiply plus the 7-bit final add — less soft logic than a DSP tile costs — and it would place a 2.14 ns combinational block between the i12* registers and the module outputs r/g/b, which drive combinational mux logic in the parent (lines 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484) before reaching a register, lengthening that stage. Both effects push area × delay the wrong way, so they stay in soft logic.

Block count — the rewrite adds 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of the 132 available. It removes six soft 8×7 multipliers, three 15-bit adder inputs (the sums go from 3-term to 2-term) and 42 flip-flops (six 15-bit product registers replaced by three 16-bit registers), which is more soft area than the three DSP tiles cost. The longest new path is ul/rul flop → block → i12* flop ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 = 2.20 ns, ending at a register and shorter than the 16×16 soft multiplies in matmult (lines 335–343) that set this design's critical path, so clock period should not regress.

Simulation

When you finish, the framework simulates your edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles), on its own copy. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/; you may read them, but editing them has no effect on the result.

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Inner-loop check: the front end of the real flow, then a cell census. NOT place-and-route.
# usage: bash check.sh <file.v>
# Reads, in the flow's order: blocks.vlib (every hard block of the architecture, declared from its
# models), primitives.vlib (the flow's library of memories, adders and multipliers), then the design;
# then elaborates with the flow's hierarchy check. A module that is not defined, a port name the
# block does not have, or a misspelled block name fails here as it would in the flow. Do not
# declare the target block in the design: blocks.vlib declares it, and a design that defines a module
# of the target block's name fails this check.
# Prints one pass/fail line, then the cell census of the whole design (the last "Number of cells"
# table yosys prints): soft arithmetic as $mul/$add/$sub, registers as $dff*/$sdff*, memories as
# $mem*, muxes as $mux/$pmux, and every instantiated module by name, which is where a hard-block
# instantiation shows up with its instance count.
set -u
f="${1:?usage: check.sh <file.v>}"
here="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
lib=""
[ -f "$here/blocks.vlib" ] && lib="read_verilog -lib $here/blocks.vlib; "
[ -f "$here/primitives.vlib" ] && lib="${lib}read_verilog -nomem2reg $here/primitives.vlib; "
target="$(sed -n 's|^// target block: *\([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_$]*\).*|\1|p' "$here/blocks.vlib" 2>/dev/null | head -1)"
if [ -n "$target" ] && grep -Eq "^[[:space:]]*module[[:space:]]+$target([^A-Za-z0-9_$]|$)" "$f"; then
  echo "FAIL: $f defines module $target; blocks.vlib declares it. Remove the module definition and keep the instances."
  exit 1
fi
out="$(yosys -p "${lib}read_verilog -sv -nolatches $f; hierarchy -check -auto-top -purge_lib; proc; opt; stat" 2>&1)"
rc=$?
if [ $rc -ne 0 ]; then
  echo "FAIL: does not synthesize (yosys exit $rc)"
  echo "$out" | grep -iE 'error|warning' | tail -15
  echo "$out" | tail -10
  exit 1
fi
echo "OK: synthesizes cleanly"
echo "Cell census (yosys stat, whole design):"
echo "$out" | awk '/Number of cells:/{buf=$0 "\n"; on=1; next}
                   on && /^[[:space:]]+[$A-Za-z_][^[:space:]]*[[:space:]]+[0-9]+[[:space:]]*$/ {buf=buf $0 "\n"; next}
                   on {on=0}
                   END{printf "%s", buf}' \
  | grep -E 'Number of cells|^[[:space:]]+(\$(mul|add|sub|mem|memrd|memwr|dff|dffe|sdff|sdffe|adff|adffe|mux|pmux)|[A-Za-z_])' || true
===
// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
// input reaches result in the same cycle.
module comb_sop_2_18 (
    input  [17:0] ax,
    input  [18:0] ay,
    input  [17:0] bx,
    input  [18:0] by,
    output [63:0] result
);

    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact

    assign result = sum;

endmodule
===

Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file raygentop.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.

  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The approved plan

I'll start by reading the design source around the candidate regions.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = ul (8b, zero-extend to 18), ay = rul (7b, zero-extend to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = rvl (7b → 19), result → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g: ax = ul, ay = gul, bx = vl, by = gvl, result → new reg i12g
  • block sop_b: ax = ul, ay = bul, bx = vl, by = bvl, result → new reg i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What changes — in module bilinearintrp (lines 2762–2887 of raygentop.v) only. Nothing outside the module is touched.

Each colour channel currently is: nine registered products (lines 2874–2882), then a 3-term combinational sum truncated to 7 bits (lines 2884–2886). For each channel, the two products on ul and vl move into one comb_sop_2_18, whose combinational sum is registered in place of the two separate product registers. The wl product keeps its soft multiplier and its register, and the final adder becomes a 2-term add.

Edits

  1. Delete the declarations of i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2799–2807 keep only i3b, i3g, i3r). Add:
    ```verilog
    reg [15:0] i12r;
    reg [15:0] i12g;
    reg [15:0] i12b;
    wire [63:0] sopr, sopg, sopb;
    ```
  2. Instantiate the three blocks at module scope (outside the always blocks), with explicit zero-extension so the unsigned widening is visible:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, rul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, rvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, gul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, gvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, bul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, bvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```
  3. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883): delete lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881; keep 2876, 2879, 2882 (i3r <= wl * rwl; i3g <= wl * gwl; i3b <= wl * bwl;) exactly as they are; add
    ```verilog
    i12r <= sopr[15:0];
    i12g <= sopg[15:0];
    i12b <= sopb[15:0];
    ```
    Keep every other assignment in that block (wl, ul, vl, rul … bwl) unchanged — they are the block's operands and wl/ul/vl have other readers.
  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r + i3r);
    assign g = (i12g + i3g);
    assign b = (i12b + i3b);
    ```

Why this is the same behaviour

  • Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bwl), matching the block's unsigned reading; zero-extension to 18/19 bits is value-preserving. Each product is at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum at most 64770, so the 64-bit result is exact and sopX[15:0] is the full sum — no wrap, no saturation to consider.
  • Timing: at a clock edge in cycle t, sopr is a pure function of the pre-edge ul, rul, vl, rvl, exactly what lines 2874–2875 multiplied, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1). i3r is untouched. Hence r = i12r + i3r equals i1r + i2r + i3r on every cycle, truncated to 7 bits identically (truncation is distributive over addition mod 2^7).
  • Visibility: i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are read nowhere except lines 2884–2886 (checked across the whole file), so folding them into the blocks hides nothing another reader needs. ul, vl and the colour registers stay outside the blocks as real registers, so their other readers are unaffected.
  • Registers: the block has none and needs no reset; i12r/i12g/i12b replace i1*/i2* at the same pipeline position. They power up unknown, but the module has no reset port and the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in clocks them to a defined value before comparison starts (comparison begins at the end of the lead-in), and they resolve no later than the original i1r/i2r did.
  • No assumption about the design's inputs is required; there is no ASSUMES: line.

Register stages, per converted region — before: operands ul/vl/rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*) → combinational sum → output. After: operands → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational add with i3* → output. One stage before and one after; output latency from module inputs is unchanged.

Candidates left unconverted — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl products). Each is the third product of a 3-product chain whose first two already fill one block. Putting each in a block of its own would need a second DSP tile per channel (253779 MWTA ≈ 9.1 CLBs) to absorb one 8×7 multiply plus the 7-bit final add — less soft logic than a DSP tile costs — and it would place a 2.14 ns combinational block between the i12* registers and the module outputs r/g/b, which drive combinational mux logic in the parent (lines 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484) before reaching a register, lengthening that stage. Both effects push area × delay the wrong way, so they stay in soft logic.

Block count — the rewrite adds 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of the 132 available. It removes six soft 8×7 multipliers, three 15-bit adder inputs (the sums go from 3-term to 2-term) and 42 flip-flops (six 15-bit product registers replaced by three 16-bit registers), which is more soft area than the three DSP tiles cost. The longest new path is ul/rul flop → block → i12* flop ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 = 2.20 ns, ending at a register and shorter than the 16×16 soft multiplies in matmult (lines 335–343) that set this design's critical path, so clock period should not regress.

DESIGN_CHANGE: matmult (lines 335–350) computes nine 16×16 products summed three per output (Cx, Cy, Cz); those operands fit ax[18]/ay[19] directly and are the design's dominant arithmetic and likely critical path. Exposing them as candidate regions would let the block displace far more soft logic than bilinearintrp's 8×7 products do.

Simulation

simulation passed: 1052 cycles, 0 mismatches. The result (sim_result.json) and log (sim_run.log) are attached. Simulation shows that every output of bilinearintrp matches on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles); it does not show that the edit follows the plan or the correctness rules. That is your check.

The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v, sim_result.json, sim_run.log

response

I'll read the source and supporting files.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan and the rules:

  • Plan conformance. The six converted regions are exactly the ones the plan names: the ul*rul, vl*rvl, ul*gul, vl*gvl, ul*bul, vl*bvl products (original lines 2874/2875, 2877/2878, 2880/2881) are gone, and the three wl*… products (i3r/i3g/i3b, raygentop.v:2878-2880) are kept verbatim as the plan says. Declarations of i1*/i2* removed, i12r/i12g/i12b and sopr/sopg/sopb added (raygentop.v:2799-2807), three blocks at module scope (raygentop.v:2818-2820), final sums reduced to two terms (raygentop.v:2885-2887). The reordering of the nonblocking assignments inside always @(posedge clk) is semantically inert. No change outside module bilinearintrp; the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms that.
  • Ports. All four operand ports plus result connected on each of the three instances, by name, with widths exact: {10'b0, ul} = 18, {12'b0, rul} = 19, etc. No port is tied to 0, matching the plan; the model (sim_reference/comb_sop_2_18.v:8-12) agrees on widths and is purely combinational with no reset, so the contract needs no reset drive.
  • Same behavior. Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bvl), so zero-extension is value-preserving. Each product ≤ 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum ≤ 64770 < 2^16, so sop*[15:0] is the exact i1*+i2* and the 64-bit result never wraps. sop* depends only on pre-edge ul/vl/rul/rvl/…, the same values the deleted products sampled, so i12x(t+1) = i1x(t+1) + i2x(t+1) and the pipeline depth is unchanged. The final sum moves from 15-bit to 16-bit modular addition, but r/g/b take only the low 7 bits, which are identical mod 2^7.
  • Visibility and terms. i1r/i2r/i1g/i2g/i1b/i2b had no reader anywhere in the file outside the deleted sums (grep over /work). ul, vl and the colour registers remain real registers outside the blocks. All nine original terms survive: six in the blocks, three in soft logic.
  • Registers/reset. The block holds none. i12* are added registers; they are written unconditionally at the first edge and nothing relies on their power-up value, consistent with the rule and with x_on_dut_only: 0 in sim_result.json.
  • Assumptions. None required, and none claimed — correct, since no operand bound is needed to rule out wrap.

1	// comb_sop_2_18: combinational, no clock. result = ax*ay + bx*by, all four operands unsigned
2	// (decision 2026-10-16: every integer mode on this arch is unsigned); each 18x19 product is 37
3	// bits and the sum is formed at 64 bits, so result is exact and never wraps (the 64-bit result
4	// wraps only in principle: no sum of these operands reaches it).
5	// Ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19] in; result[64] out. No registers and no reset: every
6	// input reaches result in the same cycle.
7	module comb_sop_2_18 (
8	    input  [17:0] ax,
9	    input  [18:0] ay,
10	    input  [17:0] bx,
11	    input  [18:0] by,
12	    output [63:0] result
13	);
14	
15	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax * ay;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
16	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx * by;                                    // 18x19 -> 37, exact
17	    wire [63:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b;                            // zero-extended, exact
18	
19	    assign result = sum;
20	
21	endmodule

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: comb_sop_2_18 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by
  • input ports: ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19]
  • output ports: result[64]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: none; the block is combinational, and result is valid in the same cycle the inputs are applied.
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
block, input to output2.14
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • The block has no output register, so the logic its output drives is in the same stage.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

id
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. raygentop.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r: ax = ul (8b, zero-extend to 18), ay = rul (7b, zero-extend to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = rvl (7b → 19), result → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g: ax = ul, ay = gul, bx = vl, by = gvl, result → new reg i12g
  • block sop_b: ax = ul, ay = bul, bx = vl, by = bvl, result → new reg i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What changes — in module bilinearintrp (lines 2762–2887 of raygentop.v) only. Nothing outside the module is touched.

Each colour channel currently is: nine registered products (lines 2874–2882), then a 3-term combinational sum truncated to 7 bits (lines 2884–2886). For each channel, the two products on ul and vl move into one comb_sop_2_18, whose combinational sum is registered in place of the two separate product registers. The wl product keeps its soft multiplier and its register, and the final adder becomes a 2-term add.

Edits

  1. Delete the declarations of i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b (lines 2799–2807 keep only i3b, i3g, i3r). Add:
    ```verilog
    reg [15:0] i12r;
    reg [15:0] i12g;
    reg [15:0] i12b;
    wire [63:0] sopr, sopg, sopb;
    ```
  2. Instantiate the three blocks at module scope (outside the always blocks), with explicit zero-extension so the unsigned widening is visible:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, rul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, rvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, gul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, gvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, bul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, bvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```
  3. In the always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883): delete lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881; keep 2876, 2879, 2882 (i3r <= wl * rwl; i3g <= wl * gwl; i3b <= wl * bwl;) exactly as they are; add
    ```verilog
    i12r <= sopr[15:0];
    i12g <= sopg[15:0];
    i12b <= sopb[15:0];
    ```
    Keep every other assignment in that block (wl, ul, vl, rul … bwl) unchanged — they are the block's operands and wl/ul/vl have other readers.
  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r + i3r);
    assign g = (i12g + i3g);
    assign b = (i12b + i3b);
    ```

Why this is the same behaviour

  • Operands are unsigned (reg[7:0] ul/vl, reg[6:0] rul…bwl), matching the block's unsigned reading; zero-extension to 18/19 bits is value-preserving. Each product is at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block sum at most 64770, so the 64-bit result is exact and sopX[15:0] is the full sum — no wrap, no saturation to consider.
  • Timing: at a clock edge in cycle t, sopr is a pure function of the pre-edge ul, rul, vl, rvl, exactly what lines 2874–2875 multiplied, so i12r(t+1) = i1r(t+1) + i2r(t+1). i3r is untouched. Hence r = i12r + i3r equals i1r + i2r + i3r on every cycle, truncated to 7 bits identically (truncation is distributive over addition mod 2^7).
  • Visibility: i1r, i2r, i1g, i2g, i1b, i2b are read nowhere except lines 2884–2886 (checked across the whole file), so folding them into the blocks hides nothing another reader needs. ul, vl and the colour registers stay outside the blocks as real registers, so their other readers are unaffected.
  • Registers: the block has none and needs no reset; i12r/i12g/i12b replace i1*/i2* at the same pipeline position. They power up unknown, but the module has no reset port and the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in clocks them to a defined value before comparison starts (comparison begins at the end of the lead-in), and they resolve no later than the original i1r/i2r did.
  • No assumption about the design's inputs is required; there is no ASSUMES: line.

Register stages, per converted region — before: operands ul/vl/rul/rvl/gul/gvl/bul/bvl → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*) → combinational sum → output. After: operands → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational add with i3* → output. One stage before and one after; output latency from module inputs is unchanged.

Candidates left unconverted — bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356 (the wl*rwl, wl*gwl, wl*bwl products). Each is the third product of a 3-product chain whose first two already fill one block. Putting each in a block of its own would need a second DSP tile per channel (253779 MWTA ≈ 9.1 CLBs) to absorb one 8×7 multiply plus the 7-bit final add — less soft logic than a DSP tile costs — and it would place a 2.14 ns combinational block between the i12* registers and the module outputs r/g/b, which drive combinational mux logic in the parent (lines 2265–2267, 2423–2425, 2482–2484) before reaching a register, lengthening that stage. Both effects push area × delay the wrong way, so they stay in soft logic.

Block count — the rewrite adds 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of the 132 available. It removes six soft 8×7 multipliers, three 15-bit adder inputs (the sums go from 3-term to 2-term) and 42 flip-flops (six 15-bit product registers replaced by three 16-bit registers), which is more soft area than the three DSP tiles cost. The longest new path is ul/rul flop → block → i12* flop ≈ 0.060 + 2.14 = 2.20 ns, ending at a register and shorter than the 16×16 soft multiplies in matmult (lines 335–343) that set this design's critical path, so clock period should not regress.

DESIGN_CHANGE: matmult (lines 335–350) computes nine 16×16 products summed three per output (Cx, Cy, Cz); those operands fit ax[18]/ay[19] directly and are the design's dominant arithmetic and likely critical path. Exposing them as candidate regions would let the block displace far more soft logic than bilinearintrp's 8×7 products do.

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (comb_sop_2_18.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the design source and the relevant regions before deciding.

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: module bilinearintrp — three separate sums, one block each: r (line 2884), g (2885), b (2886)
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • block sop_r_i: ax = ul (8b, zero-extended to 18), ay = drul = new 7-bit reg holding ru - rw (zero-extended to 19), bx = vl (8b → 18), by = drvl = new 7-bit reg holding rv - rw (→ 19), result[6:0] → new reg i12r
  • block sop_g_i: ax = ul, ay = dgul (gu - gw), bx = vl, by = dgvl (gv - gw), result[6:0] → i12g
  • block sop_b_i: ax = ul, ay = dbul (bu - bw), bx = vl, by = dbvl (bv - bw), result[6:0] → i12b
  • no port is tied to 0; all four product-term ports are used on all three blocks

PLAN:

What this changes relative to the kept candidate. The kept candidate put six of the nine products in three blocks and left the three wl * *wl products as soft multipliers, costing a DSP tile of extra area (+5.92%) for its −6.67% period. This revision keeps the same three blocks and the same critical-path shape, and removes the three remaining multipliers, the wl register and its two 8-bit subtracts entirely, by folding the wl term algebraically into the block's two products plus a 7-bit constant correction. The block path (flop → block → flop), which is the measured critical path, is untouched: the block's operands are still register outputs and its result still goes straight to a register. The new subtracters sit on paths from ru/rv/rw, which come from shadedata, i.e. straight from the tm3_sram_data_in pads (line 676: assign shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in;, carried to line 2012), so they are on pad-to-register paths, not on any register-to-register path.

The identity. Only the low 7 bits of each sum are used: r is a 7-bit wire assigned the 15-bit sum (line 2884), so r = (i1r + i2r + i3r) mod 128. With U = ul, V = vl, W = wl = (255 − U − V) mod 256, A = rul, B = rvl, C = rwl (all stage-1 values of the same cycle):

  • W*C = (255 − U − V)*C + 256k*C for some integer k, and 256k*C ≡ 0 (mod 128), so the 8-bit wrap of line 2862 is irrelevant mod 128 — no assumption on u + v ≤ 255 is needed.
  • r ≡ U*A + V*B + 255*C − U*C − V*C ≡ U*(A−C) + V*(B−C) + 255*C (mod 128), and 255*C = 256C − C ≡ −C (mod 128).
  • Replacing A−C by its 7-bit wrap DA = (A−C) mod 128 changes the product by a multiple of 128, so U*DA ≡ U*(A−C) (mod 128).

Hence r = (U*DA + V*DB − C) mod 128 exactly, for all inputs. The block computes U*DA + V*DB (unsigned, max 2·255·127 = 64770, far below 2^64, so no wrap and result[6:0] is the true sum mod 128), and −C is a 7-bit subtract outside the block.

Pipeline alignment. DA must be formed from ru and rw of the same cycle, so it is computed before the stage-1 register: drul <= ru - rw gives drul(t−1) = ru(t−2) − rw(t−2) = A − C with A = rul(t−1), C = rwl(t−1) — the same values the original multiplied. The correction term needs C = rwl(t−1) = rw(t−2) at time t, which rwl alone does not hold, so a second delay stage rwl2 <= rwl is added (likewise gwl2, bwl2). The correction is applied after the i12* register, not before it, so that nothing is added between the block output and its capture flop.

Edits — module bilinearintrp only (lines 2762–2887). Nothing outside the module is touched.

  1. Declarations (lines 2798–2816): delete reg[7:0] wl;, all nine i1*/i2*/i3* regs, and rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl. Keep u, v, ul, vl, rwl, gwl, bwl. Add:
    ```verilog
    reg[6:0] drul; reg[6:0] drvl;
    reg[6:0] dgul; reg[6:0] dgvl;
    reg[6:0] dbul; reg[6:0] dbvl;
    reg[6:0] rwl2; reg[6:0] gwl2; reg[6:0] bwl2;
    reg[6:0] i12r; reg[6:0] i12g; reg[6:0] i12b;
    wire[63:0] sopr; wire[63:0] sopg; wire[63:0] sopb;
    ```
  2. At module scope (outside the always blocks), instantiate the three blocks with explicit zero-extension:
    ```verilog
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_r_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, drul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, drvl}), .result(sopr));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_g_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dgul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dgvl}), .result(sopg));
    comb_sop_2_18 sop_b_i (.ax({10'b0, ul}), .ay({12'b0, dbul}), .bx({10'b0, vl}), .by({12'b0, dbvl}), .result(sopb));
    ```

  3. The always @(posedge clk) block (2860–2883) becomes exactly:

    verilog
      always @(posedge clk)
  begin
     ul <= u ;
     vl <= v ;
     drul <= ru - rw ;
     drvl <= rv - rw ;
     dgul <= gu - gw ;
     dgvl <= gv - gw ;
     dbul <= bu - bw ;
     dbvl <= bv - bw ;
     rwl <= rw ;
     gwl <= gw ;
     bwl <= bw ;
     rwl2 <= rwl ;
     gwl2 <= gwl ;
     bwl2 <= bwl ;
     i12r <= sopr[6:0] ;
     i12g <= sopg[6:0] ;
     i12b <= sopb[6:0] ;
  end

    (line 2862 wl <= 8'b11111111 - u - v ; is deleted; wl has no other reader). Each ru - rw is a 7-bit-wide assignment, so it wraps mod 128, which is what the identity requires.

  4. Replace lines 2884–2886 with
    ```verilog
    assign r = (i12r - rwl2) ;
    assign g = (i12g - gwl2) ;
    assign b = (i12b - bwl2) ;
    ```
    r/g/b are 7-bit wires and both operands are 7-bit, so the subtract wraps mod 128, as required.
  5. The combinational u/v mux (2818–2858) is unchanged.

Visibility. i1r…i3b were read only by lines 2884–2886; rul, rvl, gul, gvl, bul, bvl only by the products; wl only by 2876/2879/2882; ul, vl only by the products (grep over the whole file confirms all of these are module-local with no other reader). ul and vl remain real registers outside the blocks. rwl, gwl, bwl remain registers and now feed the second delay stage. Nothing that another reader needs is hidden inside a block.

Registers and reset. The blocks have no registers and no reset. i12* sits at the same pipeline position as the old i1*/i2*/i3*; drul…dbvl replace rul…bvl at stage 1; rwl2/gwl2/bwl2 are a second copy of an existing delay. Latency from module inputs to r/g/b is 2 register stages before and after — unchanged. The module has no reset port; the new registers power up unknown and are flushed to their defined values within 2 cycles of the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in, before comparison starts (all-zero inputs drive every one of them to 0, matching the original's registers). No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is exact for every input, including when u + v > 255 wraps wl.

Register stages per converted region. For all nine products: before — operands (ul,vl,wl and the nine colour regs) → 1 register stage (i1*,i2*,i3*) → combinational 3-term sum → output. After — operands (ul,vl,drul,drvl,…) → block (combinational) → 1 register stage (i12*) → combinational 7-bit subtract with the equally-delayed *wl2 → output. One stage before the sum and one after, both before and after the rewrite.

Every candidate, converted or left. All nine are converted; none is left in a soft multiplier. Six of them (:2874$348, :2875$349, :2877$351, :2878$352, :2880$354, :2881$355) are the block's literal product terms, with their ay/by operand rebiased from *ul/*vl to *ul − *wl / *vl − *wl. The other three (:2876$350, :2879$353, :2882$356, the wl*rwl/gwl/bwl products) are folded into those same blocks by the distribution above: their −ul*rwl and −vl*rwl parts ride in the block's two products through the rebiased operands, and their remaining 255*rwl ≡ −rwl part is the 7-bit subtract at the output. Their multipliers disappear; no product term of the original arithmetic is dropped.

Block count and expected effect. 3 blocks, i.e. 3 DSP tiles of 132 — the same count the kept candidate packed, so the DSP-side area is unchanged and the measured block path (flop 0.060 → routing → block 2.140 → routing → flop, 4.49 ns at seed 1) keeps the same endpoints and the same operand widths; the result net is now 7 bits instead of 16, so if anything the block's output routing is lighter. Against the kept candidate this removes three 8×7 multipliers (which, with i3* trimmed to its used 7 bits, are what the 4th non-block DSP tile or an equivalent block of soft logic holds), the 8-bit wl register and the two 8-bit subtracts feeding it, and 9 bits × 3 of the i12* registers; it adds six 7-bit subtracters on untimed pad paths and three 7-bit delay registers. That is strictly less logic than the kept candidate at the same block count and the same critical path, so area × delay should fall below the kept −1.15%.

DESIGN_CHANGE: matmult (lines 335–350) computes nine 16×16 products summed three per output (Cx, Cy, Cz); those operands fit ax[18]/ay[19] directly and are the design's largest arithmetic. Offering them as candidate regions would let the block displace far more soft logic than bilinearintrp's 8×7 products, whose results are truncated to 7 bits and are therefore cheap in soft logic to begin with.
